<!-- session 2026-10-22 node-runtime-pins -->

> **Ad-hoc session (2026-10-22), `main` on `dcd4f8d7`, Node runtime pins after the Node.js 21 October 2026 security release (fixed in 22.23.4 and 24.22.1): `engines.node` and the CI `setup-node` versions moved off Node 18 and 20, EDITED and checked, NOT committed, NOT pushed, NOT deployed.** Davin asked whether the release concerns this codebase. It does (the exposure analysis is in the gitignored security checklist, §6.1). The change list was proposed first and applied on his "Apply it". Davin reported that Railway has an outage and a security incident and that deploys are paused: nothing in this session connected to Railway.
> **Needs from Davin:** (1) Commit and push, when he wants. (2) **After Railway deploys resume, read `node -v` in each service's build log.** Nixpacks maps `22.x` to a major only (the patch comes from a nixpkgs snapshot compiled into Nixpacks; read in its `main` source, not in the version Railway runs), so the repo cannot force 22.23.4 there; if the log shows an older patch the choice is Railpack (`RAILPACK_NODE_VERSION`) or a Dockerfile (`FROM node:22.23.4`), a deploy-tooling decision. (3) **Sign-off on `22.x` for `money-service`** (payments): which major Railway used before was never confirmed; with `>=20.0.0` Nixpacks takes the highest even major it knows (24 on current Nixpacks, 22 on older ones), so `22.x` is a downgrade if it was 24. If the build log shows 24, set the three services to `24.x` and move `nestjs-services-ci.yml` to 24. (4) **The chat stack's Dockerfiles now say `node:22-alpine` in the repo** (`infra/contabo-chat-stack/apps/server/Dockerfile` and `apps/bot-worker/Dockerfile`, both stages of each; `docker-compose.dev.yml:41` too), at his word, repo only: **nothing was rebuilt or deployed and the VPS was not touched**; the VPS picks the new image up only through the runbook rebuild (`docs/runbooks/contabo-chat-stack.md`). A build on Node 22 was not run; neither app lists a native module. (5) First CI run: the monolith workflows have never run on Node 24 (`mt5-pipeline-tests` and `openapi-validation` moved from 18 to 22).
> **Changed & verified:** `package.json`, `frontend/package.json`, `packages/types/package.json`: `engines.node` `>=20.0.0` to `>=22.0.0`; `money-service`, `operation-service`, `railway-gateway` `package.json`: to `22.x`, and the same value in the root entry of each service's `package-lock.json` (hand edit, nothing regenerated). CI: Node 20 to 24 in `api-tests`, `bundle-monitor`, `check-production-migrations`, `ci-nextjs-progressive` (one variable, five jobs), `dependencies-security`, `e2e-tests` (2), `lockfile-regen`, `security-checks` (2) and `tests` (the matrix and 2 jobs); Node 18 to 22 in `mt5-pipeline-tests` (3) and `openapi-validation`; `nestjs-services-ci.yml` was already 22. Images: `node:20-alpine` to `node:22-alpine` in both stages of the two chat-stack Dockerfiles and in `docker-compose.dev.yml:41` (5 lines; diff and line endings checked; the only `node:20` left in Dockerfiles and compose files are two read-only `seed-code/**` Dockerfiles). No `nixpacks.toml` change on purpose (`engines.node` outranks everything else Nixpacks reads). Vercel needs no change: `>=22.0.0` still maps to the latest 24.x (Vercel docs) and Vercel rolls patch releases itself. Evidence: `git diff --numstat` shows one line added and one removed per edited line (21 workflow lines in 11 files, six `package.json`); git treats the three lockfiles as binary, so each was read back (`"node": "22.x"` in the root entry); `JSON.parse` of all nine JSON files passes; each file kept its own line endings; no test, script or config reads `node-version` or `engines` (Grep). Not run: `test:ci`, `tsc`, the build and every workflow (configuration only).
> **Unconfirmed / found:** item 2; the other open checks of this release are in the gitignored security checklist (§6.1). `seed-code/stack-auth/docker/**` still has two `node:20-alpine` Dockerfiles (read-only, not changed). `@types/node` stays `^20.19.27` (root) and `^22.10.2` (services): types only, and a change needs a lockfile run. The untracked root `package-lock.json` was not touched. Local Node is 24.12.0 and does not satisfy `22.x` in the services (npm warns; `engine-strict` is off).

> **Needs from Davin:** (1) **Look at the app signed in, in dark and light, with the amber accent and one other accent.** The token change is app-wide: classes such as `bg-muted/40`, `border-border/50`, `bg-accent/50`, `bg-primary/10`, `bg-background/95` and the hover states (`hover:bg-primary/90` on every `Button`, `hover:bg-accent/50`) were dead and now render. Likely visible: button hover, sticky headers, tinted alert and status boxes, the PRO plan card tint, the usage `Progress` track. Name any page that looks wrong. (2) A decision still open: the FREE plan card and its "FREE TIER" badge use the neutral `--border` / `--muted` tokens (slate-blue in dark) on purpose; if he wants them to follow the accent that is a design change. (3) The commit and the push, when he wants them (the pre-commit hook re-sorts the classes of each staged file).
> **Changed & verified:** `tailwind.config.ts`: all 37 colour tokens are `oklch(var(--x) / <alpha-value>)`. `app/settings/billing/page.tsx`: the banner `Card` is `from-primary to-primary/80` (was `to-amber-600`), the button text is `text-zinc-900` on its `bg-white` (was `text-foreground`, near-white in dark mode), the description line is `opacity-90` (was a dead `text-primary-foreground/90`); prettier re-sorted three other class lists in that file. `environment-gotchas.md` carries the trap. Evidence: a Tailwind CLI compile of the whole project (`app/globals.css`), before and after: 1,653 to 1,736 selectors, **83 added, 0 removed**, `.bg-primary` now `oklch(var(--primary) / var(--tw-bg-opacity, 1))`; the CSS that `next dev` served for `/pricing` holds `.bg-primary/20`, `.bg-primary/5`, `.border-border/50`, `.hover:bg-primary/90`, `.bg-background/95` and `.to-primary/80`; `eslint` and `prettier --check --end-of-line auto` clean on the two changed files; `__tests__/pages/settings/billing.test.tsx` 12 of 12; root `npm run type-check` exit 0 (run before the config change, which edits strings only).
> **Unconfirmed / found:** not run: a browser check (the browser pane refuses `http://localhost:3000`, and the real pages are behind login), the full Jest suite, `npm run build`, `type-check` after the config edit. `prettier --check .` now flags class order in files written before the fix, because the plugin recognises these classes at last; `format:check` is non-blocking in CI and I ran `--write` only on the two changed files; how many files differ was not counted. The other Tailwind configs (`frontend/`, `mobile-app/`, `seed-code/`) were not touched. `next dev` rewrote `next-env.d.ts`; restored with `git checkout`, and the dev server was stopped. The images were read as: image 1 dark, image 2 light, the invisible button text being the defect that shows.

<!-- session 2026-10-20 operation-service-dynamic-prices -->

> **Ad-hoc session (2026-10-20), `main` on `82adc7cd` (COMMITTED as `74797dbe` and PUSHED to `origin/main` by Davin, NOT deployed, no migration): operation-service no longer prints a PRO price of its own. The $29 / $290 hardcodes are gone: money-service now puts `priceUsd` (and `billingPeriod`) in the `TIER_UPGRADED` and `PAYMENT_FAILED` outbox events and the consumer passes it to the e-mails; BUILT and verified locally on Davin's pasted task (it closes item (5) of the localized-emails entry below; no migration order).**
> **Needs from Davin:** (1) Nothing to commit or push: he committed and pushed `74797dbe`. (2) **Deploy money-service BEFORE operation-service** (the two were independent before; for these two events they are not): the new consumer skips a `TIER_UPGRADED` or `PAYMENT_FAILED` event that has no usable `priceUsd` (status `skipped`, reason `missing-price`, a warning, no e-mail), so an old money-service, or an event queued before the deploy, loses that one e-mail instead of sending a wrong price. (3) A look at `money-service/src/stripe/stripe-webhook.service.ts` and `src/dlocal/dlocal-webhook.controller.ts` (payment files): only fields were added to outbox payloads, no payment logic was touched; two of the additions go beyond the brief (see Unconfirmed). (4) A decision: the dLocal `THREE_DAY` plan has no wording of its own in the confirmation template, so its e-mail prints the 3-day price under "/month" (before: the $29 list price under "/month", also wrong); a fix is a new localized text in 19 languages.
> **Changed & verified:** money-service: the Stripe `TIER_UPGRADED` carries `priceUsd` = the SystemConfig price checkout charged (`amountUsd`; not `invoice.amount_paid`, which is $0 in a trial and includes tax); `PAYMENT_FAILED` carries `priceUsd` = the subscriber's own `Subscription.amountUsd` (falling back to the SystemConfig price of the plan) and `billingPeriod`, as `lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.ts` does; the dLocal `TIER_UPGRADED` carries `priceUsd` = `planAmountUsd` and `billingPeriod` (yearly for a `YEARLY` plan, else monthly). operation-service: `OutboxConsumerService` reads `priceUsd` (a finite number above 0) and passes it on, with `billingPeriod` for the failed-payment e-mail; `email.util.ts` lost `LEGACY_PRO_PRICE_USD`; `priceUsd` is required and sits before `locale` in `getSubscriptionConfirmationEmail` and `sendSubscriptionConfirmationEmail` (the monolith's order); `getTrialReminderEmail`, `getUpgradePromptEmail` and their senders take `monthlyPriceUsd` (a local `formatEmailUsd`, as in the monolith; the old "$29/month or $290/year" lines are now one price); `subscription-email.util.ts`: `price` is required and `billingPeriod` (default monthly) is a parameter of `getPaymentFailedEmailTemplate` and `sendPaymentFailedEmail`. `lib/email/i18n/` and its operation-service copy were not touched (`mirror.test.ts` passes). Evidence: root `npm run type-check` exit 0; operation-service `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; money-service `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (in a scratch copy, see below); operation-service `src/email` + `src/outbox` 9 suites, 81 tests; **full operation-service 63 suites, 638 tests, 0 failed**; money-service `src/stripe` + `src/outbox` + `src/dlocal` 14 suites, 260 tests, 0 failed; root `__tests__/lib/email` 8 suites, 583 tests (includes `mirror.test.ts`); `prettier --check --end-of-line auto` clean on the 10 changed files (one spec reformatted).
> **Unconfirmed / found:** no mutation pass (the new assertions use exact payload equality, but the old code was not run against them); not run: the full monolith Jest, `eslint`, a real send. money-service has NO `node_modules` in this checkout: its tests ran in a scratch copy (`C:\Users\k_kav\AppData\Local\Temp\nci\money-service`, a `robocopy` of the working tree and `npm ci`, 38 s), left in place and safe to delete. Beyond the brief: `billingPeriod` was added to the `PAYMENT_FAILED` and dLocal events (without it a yearly subscriber would be shown `$290/month`), and a missing price makes the consumer skip (it has no price to fall back on). Whether the outbox publisher is enabled in production was not checked (the consumer's own comment says it carried no traffic until 4A-12). The "alert triggered" template is still English only and has no caller.

<!-- session 2026-10-20 localized-emails -->

> **Ad-hoc session (2026-10-20), `main` on `de61737e` (working tree, NOT committed, NOT pushed, NOT deployed, no migration): every e-mail the app sends is now written in the recipient's own language, timezone, date format, time format and number format (19 locales), by the monolith AND by operation-service; BUILT and verified locally.** ([manifest](../../../davintrade-localized-emails/localized-emails-manifest-work-completion.md); policy [`08-locale-i18n-compliance.md`](../../../docs/policies/08-locale-i18n-compliance.md) §2.D.) Davin's request: the Resend e-mails (the sign-in alert, account deletion) were all English. The locale comes, field by field, from what the person saved in Settings, then the `davintrade-*` cookies of the request that triggered the mail, then English/UTC; USD stays USD (the money charged), written in the reader's number format.
> **Needs from Davin:** (1) The commit and the push, when he wants them (the pre-push hook runs the whole suite); the deploy is his call, and the two services are independent of each other. (2) **A look at `lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.ts`** (a payments file): one argument added, so the commission e-mail shows the rate the commission was computed with (`breakdown.commissionPercent`) instead of a fixed "20%"; no payment logic was touched. (3) **The translations are drafts no native reader has seen**: they need a read before billing or account-deletion wording is relied on. (4) A decision: only ONE account-deletion e-mail exists (the confirm-or-cancel request). "Deletion confirmed" and "deletion cancelled" e-mails are `TODO`s in the code and were NOT built (a new feature: two templates in 19 languages and a decision on what they may say). (5) operation-service still prints the PRO list price ($29 / $290, payment failed $29) in its subscription and payment-failed e-mails, because its outbox events carry no price: pre-existing, and the SystemConfig guard does not scan `operation-service/`; its own order.
> **Changed & verified:** new `lib/email/i18n/` (pure: 19 typed catalogs, date/time/USD formatters, the HTML builders), `lib/email/recipient-locale.ts`, and its byte-for-byte copy in `operation-service/src/email/i18n/` (a test fails on any difference) with `EmailLocaleService`, a request-hint middleware and `EmailModule` in `AppModule`. `lib/email/email.ts` and `subscription-emails.ts`, and operation-service `email.util.ts` and `subscription-email.util.ts`, are rebuilt on it (signatures kept, an optional trailing `locale`, so no caller changed). `forwardedRequestContext()` forwards `x-email-language|formats|timezone` (the three `token-*` e-mail routes forward only those); `syncLocaleCookiesAction` takes `saveToAccount` (set only for the user's own choices); `track-login` saves the browser's language once when the account has none. Evidence: root `tsc`, `eslint` and `prettier --check` on every changed file clean; **full monolith Jest 573 suites passed, 5 skipped, 0 failed (19,303 tests)**, then the touched suites re-run; **operation-service 63 suites, 627 tests**; **`npm run build` exit 0 (179/179 pages)**; sample e-mails rendered and looked at in the browser pane (Thai sign-in alert, Arabic right-to-left, German receipt, Spanish, English; one right-to-left time-reflow defect found and fixed). Two existing assertions were changed on purpose: the name is now HTML-escaped (a test asserted it raw), and `<html>` is `<html lang="…">`.
> **Unconfirmed / found:** no real Resend delivery and no signed-in click-through; `frontend/` untouched (a stale snapshot); the alert-triggered, trial-reminder and upgrade-prompt templates have no caller and stay English; PDF receipts and `session-tracker.ts` unchanged; a `UserPreferences` row can now appear at sign-in, which makes `GET /api/user/preferences` skip its geo default for `countryCode` (nothing reads that for locale today). Two gotchas recorded in `environment-gotchas.md`: the Bash tool collapses a doubled backslash (a regex written through a heredoc compiled and was wrong), and operation-service needs `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, `prisma generate` and the types build before its specs run.

<!-- session 2026-10-20 b2-pro-session-bars -->

> **Ad-hoc session (2026-10-20), `main` on `bfef7e74`, Unit 3 (B2) of the PostgreSQL/cache quick-win study: the latest-bar lookup of `getTodaysSessionBarsForIndices()` (the read behind the PRO chart, screener and detail routes) is now ONE raw SQL statement, chosen by `EXPLAIN` on a scratch PostgreSQL, BUILT and verified on Davin's APPROVED order ([order](../../../docs/migration-orders/adhoc-pro-session-bars-latest-per-index.migration-order.md): drafted by me, upgraded by the Advisor, approved by Davin in chat, CONFIRMED by me; [evidence](../../../docs/handoffs/2026-10-20-b2-explain.md)). COMMITTED on Davin's word (the commit after `bfef7e74`); he then ordered the three local commits (`6eca87f4`, `bfef7e74` and this one) pushed to `origin/main` (the outcome is in `git log origin/main`); NOT deployed.** The installed Prisma client sends `distinct` as "every historical row of the requested indices, de-duplicated in the application", and the chart and screener hooks call this about four times a minute per open cockpit.
> **Needs from Davin:** (1) Nothing to commit or push from this unit beyond what he ordered: the commit, and the push of the three local commits (check `git log origin/main`); the deploy is his call, and no migration is involved. (2) Nothing here was seen on production: the plans are from a scratch PostgreSQL **18.1** on synthetic data (production is 17.11; Docker was not available), so the timings are work done, not latency; the tables hold 0 rows today. (3) B3 (`indicator_statistics`) stays deferred; it needs its own measurement and probably an index (a DDL order Davin applies himself).
> **Changed & verified:** `lib/currency-index-pro/queries.ts`: statement (1) of `getTodaysSessionBarsForIndices()` is one constant statement through `marketPrisma.$queryRaw`: the requested names, made distinct, each with a `LATERAL ... ORDER BY bar_time DESC LIMIT 1` seek, ordered by name; the names go in as one bound `text[]` (the driver adapter accepts it, so the `Prisma.join` fallback was not needed); an empty request returns an empty `Map` without a database call. The bars read, the `Map` construction, the degrade and the three routes are unchanged. Evidence on 11,809, 662,401 and 2,010,241 rows (the same three indexes as production), for the eight FX names, one name and a hostile mix: at 2.0M rows and eight names shared buffers 287,918 for today's statement and for `DISTINCT ON`, **35** for the chosen one (0.1% of the table's pages), about 3 s against 0.3 ms; B1's recursive variant needed 69, so the simpler statement was also the cheaper one. All four statements equal the independent reference. Tests: new helper unit test `session-bars.test.ts` (9); the chart, screener and detail suites moved the lookup to `$queryRaw`, assert it is not called on a 401, 400 or 403, and each got a wiring test for the names the route sends; new gated spec `session-bars.pg.spec.ts` (**14 of 14 on a scratch PostgreSQL 18.1**) compares the new function with the old Prisma `distinct` version entry by entry and in order, including a repeated name, an absent name, an unrequested index in the table, several sessions per index and the statement count. COMMITTED on Davin's word ("commit, do NOT push yet", after the Unit 1 commit `6eca87f4`); the push of both commits was ordered together with B2's afterwards; NOT deployed.** Prisma's `distinct` sends no `DISTINCT` and no `LIMIT`, so the whole history of every index crossed the driver on each fill; a plain `DISTINCT ON` saves only that transfer and still reads every index entry and heap row.
> **Needs from Davin:** (1) The push of this commit and `6eca87f4` (Unit 1) was ordered together with B2's (check `git log origin/main`). (2) Nothing here was seen on production: the plans are from a scratch PostgreSQL **18.1** on synthetic data (production is 17.11, Docker was not available for a 17 server), so the timings are work done, not latency. B3 stays deferred.
> **Changed & verified:** `lib/currency-gold-indices/queries.ts` runs the lookup through `marketPrisma.$queryRaw` as one constant statement (a recursive walk over the distinct `index_name` values, one index seek each, then a `LATERAL ... ORDER BY bar_time DESC LIMIT 1` per name, ordered by name); the sparkline statement, the mapping, the empty early return and the degrade are unchanged, and no bind parameter is used. Evidence on 11,809, 662,401 and 2,010,241 rows (the same three indexes as production): at 2.0M rows shared buffers 324,118 for today's statement and for `DISTINCT ON`, **96** for the chosen one (0.3% of the table's pages); server time about 3.4 s against 0.6 ms; a per-name list variant is as fast but silently drops an unlisted index name (it returned 9 rows of 10) and was rejected by the order's rule. Also correct with an empty table, one group, and a table whose visibility map had not been built (131 buffers). Tests: `currency-gold-indices.test.ts` rewritten for the new call shape (18 tests, 16 before; includes the Unit 1 concurrency cases, whose counts changed); new gated spec `latest-per-index.pg.spec.ts` (9 tests, **9 of 9 on a scratch PostgreSQL 18.1**) compares the new function with the old Prisma `distinct` version over eight data shapes and counts the statements. Mutation checks: every non-equivalent mutant of the SQL and the mapping is killed by the unit suite or the gated spec; files restored byte-identical.
